\displaystyle={\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\otimes\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}+\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{~}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}=\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{~}\otimes\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}+\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{~}\otimes\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt},

(3.1.14)

yielding the spinor-gauge covariant differential as

\displaystyle d_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~}:=\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{~}\otimes\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\right)d-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}({{\mathfrak{A}}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\otimes\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt})-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{~}\otimes{{{\mathfrak{A}}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}),

(3.1.15)

The standard theory of particle physics has the
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
doublet of Dirac spinors, e.g., the left-handed electron and neutrino.
In this report, we introduce a tensor product of Weyl- and Higgs-spinorrs, namely the
dual-spinor
and define the binary operation between them as

\displaystyle\bm{\Xi}^{1}=\bm{\xi}^{1}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1},\quad\bm{\Xi}^{2}=\bm{\xi}^{2}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\implies\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}\langle\bm{\Xi}^{1},\bm{\Xi}^{2}\rangle&=&\langle\bm{\xi}^{1},\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle\hskip 1.99997pt\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\rangle\\
\langle\bm{\xi}^{1},\bm{\Xi}^{2}\rangle&=&\langle\bm{\xi}^{1},\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle\hskip 1.99997pt{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\\
\langle\bm{\Xi}^{1},\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle&=&\langle\bm{\xi}^{1},\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle\hskip 1.99997pt{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1}\\
\langle\bm{\Xi}^{1},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\rangle&=&\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\rangle\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{\xi}^{1}\\
\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1},\bm{\Xi}^{2}\rangle&=&\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}\rangle\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{\xi}^{2}\end{array}\!,\right.

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1}+\bm{\xi}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}=\bm{\xi}\otimes({\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!1}+{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}^{\!2}),\quad\bm{\xi}^{1}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}+\bm{\xi}^{2}\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}=(\bm{\xi}^{1}+\bm{\xi}^{2})\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}.

(3.1.16f)

We introduce a weak pair of undotted dual-spinors

\displaystyle{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}

\displaystyle:={\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\in\Gamma({{T\hskip-1.99997pt{\cal M}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}}},{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{e}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}},Spin(1,3)\otimes{SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)}),

(3.1.17a)

and that of dotted dual-spinors

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}

\displaystyle:=\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\in\Gamma({{T\hskip-1.99997pt{\cal M}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}}},{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{\!\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{e}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}},Spin(1,3)\otimes{SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)}),

(3.1.17b)

where superscripts “
1
” and “
2
” are labels to identify the vector spaces they belong to.
Their invariant bilinear form is

\displaystyle\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}},\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}\!,\bm{\xi}^{1}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\langle{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}\rangle+\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}\!,\bm{\xi}^{1}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\langle{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}\rangle+\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}\!,\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\langle{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\rangle+\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}\!,\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\langle{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\rangle,

\displaystyle=\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}\!,\bm{\xi}^{1}\rangle+\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}\!,\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle.

(3.1.18)

The
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
group action
\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptr}
acts on the Higgs-spinor in the dual-spinor as

\displaystyle\pi_{\hskip-0.81949pt{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}})\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}

\displaystyle=\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes({\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}})+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes({\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}),

\displaystyle=[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{1}^{\hskip 2.45836pt1}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{2}^{\hskip 2.45836pt1}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{1}^{\hskip 2.45836pt2}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{2}^{\hskip 2.45836pt2}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)\!,

\displaystyle=\left([{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{1}^{\hskip 2.45836pt1}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{1}^{\hskip 2.45836pt2}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\right)\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)+\left([{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{2}^{\hskip 2.45836pt1}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt{\dagger}}]_{2}^{\hskip 2.45836pt2}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\right)\otimes\left(\hskip-3.99994pt\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\hskip-3.99994pt\right)\!,

\displaystyle=[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt*}]^{1}_{\hskip 3.27773ptA}[\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{A}]_{1}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+[{\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}^{\hskip-2.45836pt*}]^{2}_{\hskip 3.27773ptA}[\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{A}]_{2}\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}.

(3.1.31)

When
\bm{g}
is a unitary matrix,
\bm{g}^{t}
is also a unitary matrix; thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{w}},\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{w}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}},\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\rangle,\quad\text{where}\quad\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{w}}=\pi_{\hskip-0.81949pt{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}})\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}.

(3.1.32)

The
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
generator
\bm{\tau}
and group action
\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptr}
act on the Higgs-spinor in the dual-spinor as

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{~}_{I}\cdot\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}

\displaystyle:=\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes\bm{\tau}^{~}_{I}\cdot{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes\bm{\tau}^{~}_{I}\cdot{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}},

(3.1.33)

Consequently, the dual-spinor emulates the
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
symmetry for a pair of Weyl-spinors.
The above operations with a dotted dual-spinor can easily be converted to an undotted one.
The Clifford algebra with the Lorentzian metric acts on the Weyl-spinor in the dual-spinor as

\displaystyle\gamma^{a}\bm{\Xi}=(\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\xi})\otimes{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{h}}^{~}}

\displaystyle\implies\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{*}_{\textsc{w}},\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\Xi}_{\textsc{w}}\rangle=\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1*}\!,\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\xi}^{1}\rangle+\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2*}\!,\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\xi}^{2}\rangle=\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\xi}^{1}+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}\gamma^{a}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}\bm{\xi}^{2}\!,

(3.1.34)

where
\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}:=(\bm{\xi}^{*}_{~})^{T}
​.

The spinor-gauge covariant Dirac operator acts on the dotted dual-spinor like

\displaystyle\overline{{\not\!d}}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~}:

\displaystyle End({\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{\!\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{e}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}):\dot{\bm{\Xi}}_{\textsc{w}}\mapsto\overline{{\not\!d}}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~}\dot{\bm{\Xi}}_{\textsc{w}}:=(\iota_{\bar{\gamma}}{d}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~})\dot{\bm{\Xi}}_{\textsc{w}},

\displaystyle={\not\!\partial}\dot{\bm{\Xi}}_{\textsc{w}}-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\left(({\overline{\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip 3.00003pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+({\overline{\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip 3.00003pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\right)-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\!\left(\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes(\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}})+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes(\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}})\right)\!,

\displaystyle=({\not\!\partial}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+({\not\!\partial}\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\left(({\overline{\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip 3.00003pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}}+({\overline{\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip 3.00003pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~})\otimes{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}}\right)-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}\overline{\gamma}^{\circ}\!\left(\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{1}_{~}\otimes(\bm{\tau}_{I}\cdot{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{u}}})+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{2}_{~}\otimes(\bm{\tau}_{I}\cdot{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\textsc{d}}})\right)\!,

(3.1.35)

where
\bm{\gamma_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.48297pt\textsc{l}}}^{t}=\overline{\bm{\gamma}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}
is used.

The
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
group is the internal symmetry, and the Higgs spinor does not interact directly with the spin connection.
Consequently, the gauge-covariant Dirac operator in the spinor-gauge bundle for the Higgs spinor is the same as in the gauge bundle given by (
3.1.12
).

3.2
YMU Lagrangian and equation of motion

The YMU Lagrangian form consists of five sub Lagrangians as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptmu}}={\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}+{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}+{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}+{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}+{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}\text{-}\textsc{h}},

(3.2.1)

respectively, the Yang–Mills gauge part, the gravitation part, the fermionic matter part, the Higgs part and the fermion-Higgs interaction part.
The first two parts represent gauge bosons of the Yang-Mills gauge group and the local Lorentz group.
The action integral is provided in common as

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pt\bullet}

\displaystyle:=\int_{{T^{*}\hskip-1.63885pt{\cal M}}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{l}}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\bullet},\quad\bullet\in\{\textsc{y\hskip-1.00006ptm},\textsc{g}\hskip-0.50003pt\textsc{r},\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{m}},\textsc{h},\textsc{m}\text{-}\textsc{h}\},

(3.2.2)

for each Lagrangian.
We set the Lagrangian-form and action integral to null physical dimension as
[{\mathfrak{L}}]=[\mathscr{I}]=1
in this study.

We treat the inertial Lorentzian manifold and continue to omit the subscript “
l
” in this section.

3.2.1
Gauge bosons

a) general formula:

The gauge-boson part of the YMU Lagrangian has a form in common as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G})

\displaystyle:=C^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{G}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{G}\right]=i\hskip 1.00006ptC^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{G}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\left\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}\right\|^{2}\right]\mathfrak{v},

(3.2.3)

where
C^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}
is a dimensional constant to adjust the Lagrangian to have null physical dimension.
The curvature form is defined from the connection form owing to the structure equation (
2.1.2
).
The Lagrangian form is a functional of the connection, and the equation of motion is given as the stationary point of the action integral concerning the functional variation such as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885ptG}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G})}{\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}}=0\implies\hat{d}_{G}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}:=\hat{d}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}-i\hskip 1.00006ptc_{G}\left[\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{G},\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}\right]^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\wedge}=0,

(3.2.4)

where

\displaystyle\hat{d}{\mathfrak{a}}=\text{(\ref{codiff})},~\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}\wedge{\mathfrak{a}}:=\text{det}[\bm{\eta}]^{-1}(-1)^{p(n-p+1)}\hskip 1.99997pt{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}\wedge{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mathfrak{a}})\right)\implies\int\langle{d}_{G}{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rangle\mathfrak{v}=\int\langle{\mathfrak{a}},\hat{d}_{G}{\mathfrak{b}}\rangle\mathfrak{v}.

(3.2.5)

We define
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}
through (
3.2.5
).
Except co-differential
\hat{d}
and dual-connection
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}
,
\hat{\bullet}:={\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}(\bullet)\in\Omega^{n-p}
shows Hodge-dual of
\bullet\in\Omega^{p}
.

Simple calculations provide
3.2.4
) as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{iC^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}}\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885ptG}^{~}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G})}{\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{iC^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}\frac{d}{dt}\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885ptG}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}+t{\mathfrak{a}})=\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}\frac{d}{dt}\int\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}+t{\mathfrak{a}})\|^{2}\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle=2\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}\int\left\langle\frac{d}{dt}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}+t{\mathfrak{a}}),\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}+t{\mathfrak{a}})\right\rangle\mathfrak{v}=2\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}\int\left\langle d_{G}{\mathfrak{a}},\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G})\right\rangle\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle=2\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}\int\left\langle{\mathfrak{a}},\hat{d}_{G}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G})\right\rangle\mathfrak{v}=0\implies(\ref{ELEoM}),

(3.2.6)

where we require the boundary condition as
\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{G}=0
at the integration boundary.
If and only if the curvature
\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}
fulfils the equation of motion (
3.2.4
), (
3.2.6
) is maintained for any
{\mathfrak{a}}\in\Omega^{1}{\otimes}Ad(\mathfrak{g}^{~}_{G})
.
In contrast with (
3.2.4
), the similar relation
d_{G}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{G}=0
is not the equation of motion but the Bianchi identity.

b) Yang–Mills gauge boson:

There are two gauge-bosons in the YMU theory; the Yang–Mills gauge boson and the graviton.
We start from the Yang–Mills gauge boson part;

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})

\displaystyle:=i\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\|^{2}\right]\mathfrak{v}={\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\right],

(3.2.7)

Owing to Lagrangian (
3.2.7
), we obtain the equation of motion as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pt\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})}{\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}=\dot{0}\implies\hat{d}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}=\dot{0},

(3.2.8)

where
\dot{0}
shows it is zero considering one of five sub-Lagrangians.
Since
\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885pt\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}
also has the
SU(2)
gauge field;thus, equation (
3.2.8
) is not completed yet.
After preparing all equations of motion using the whole Lagrangian, we sum them up according to the field differentiating the action.
The Yang–Mills gauge boson Lagrangian has a component representation as

(
3.2.7
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v},~~\text{where}~~\mathscr{F}^{I}_{ab}=\text{(\ref{FabISU})}.

(3.2.9)

We omit a subscript
{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptU\hskip-1.00006pt}
on the component representation for simplicity.
Consequently, the equation of motion for the Yang–Mills gauge field in a vacuum is provided with the component representations as

\displaystyle{{\eta^{~}_{\textsc{l}}}}^{\circ\circ}\left(\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\circ}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\hskip 2.45836pt{\circ}a}+c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006ptf^{I}_{\hskip 2.45836ptJK}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathscr{A}}^{J}_{\hskip 1.63885pt{\circ}}\mathscr{F}^{K}_{\hskip 2.45836pt{\circ}a}\right)\tau_{I}

\displaystyle=\dot{0}.

(3.2.10)

Physical dimension of the Yang–Mills gauge boson Lagrangian is computed as

\displaystyle\left[\partial\right]=\left[{\mathscr{A}}\right]=\left[{\mathcal{T}}\right]=L^{-1},~~\left[\mathfrak{v}\right]=L^{4}\implies\left[{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\right]=1;

thus, we set
C^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}=1
in (
3.2.7
).

c) gravitational gauge boson:

Next, we treat the Lagrangian of the gravitational gauge boson, namely the graviton;

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}({\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{e}})

\displaystyle:=-\frac{i}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}\|^{2}\right]\mathfrak{v}=-\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}\hskip-1.99997pt\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}\right],

(3.2.11)

where
\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}
is the Einstein constant of gravitation.
The cosmological constant term is omitted for simplicity in this study.
The gravitational action yields the standard Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian as shown in
Theorem 4.2
in Ref.
[
18
]
(see also,
Remark 2.1
in Ref.
[
19
]
).
Here, we shortly show the result from (
4.5.25
) using (
2.1.20a
) and (
2.1.20b
) as

\displaystyle{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}

\displaystyle=-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}}\right]=-{\mathcal{F}}^{K}_{\hskip 2.45836ptI\hskip-0.81949ptJ}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptO\hskip-0.81949pt}\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}^{I}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\mathrm{c}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{p}}^{J}\right]\Theta_{K},

\displaystyle=-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptO\hskip-0.81949pt}[\mathfrak{R}({\mathfrak{w}})\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathfrak{e}})]=\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ\circ}}({\mathfrak{w}})\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\circ\circ}}({\mathfrak{e}}),

which is nothing other than the standard Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian.
Thus, we obtain

(
3.2.11
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptO\hskip-0.81949pt}[\mathfrak{R}({\mathfrak{w}})\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathfrak{e}})]=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}R\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v},

(3.2.12)

where the rightmost expression is a component representation using the scalar curvature.
Physical dimension of the gravitational gauge boson Lagrangian is

\displaystyle\left[R\right]=\left[\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}\right]^{-1}=L^{-2},~~\left[\mathfrak{v}\right]=L^{4}\implies\left[{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}\right]=1.

Thus, we set
C^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{dim}}=(\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}})^{-1}
in (
3.2.11
).
The gravitational Lagrangian has the Planck constant to set its physical dimension null.
Although the Planck constant appears in the Lagrangian, the theory is still classical.

The gauge boson in the Yang–Mills theory corresponds to the connection in geometry; thus, we identify the spin connection
{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}
as the gravitational gauge-boson, namely the graviton.
In reality,
{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}
is a massless rank-two tensor field corresponding to the spin-two boson.
In addition to the connection, Lagrangian (
3.2.11
) is the functional of
{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}
, which is a section in the spinor-gauge bundle.
Equations of motion concerning
{\mathfrak{w}}
and
{\mathfrak{e}}
are, respectively, provided as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}({\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{e}})}{\delta{\mathfrak{e}}}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{1}{\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\mathfrak{G}^{~}_{a}=\dot{0},~~\mathfrak{G}^{~}_{a}:=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{a{\circ}{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ\circ}}\hskip-1.99997pt\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\hskip 0.81949pt{\circ}},

(3.2.13a)

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}_{\hskip-1.63885pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}({\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{e}})}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}d_{\mathfrak{w}}{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{~}=\dot{0},

(3.2.13b)

where the first is the Einstein equation, and the second is the torsion equation.

The three-form object,
\mathfrak{G}^{~}_{a}
, is called the Einstein form.
We also provide component representations of the above equations as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\mathfrak{G}^{~}_{a}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}G^{ab}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}=\dot{0},{\quad\text{with}\quad}G^{ab}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}:=R^{ab}-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{ab}\hskip 1.00006ptR,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{~}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies\frac{1}{2\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\left(\partial_{\mu}{\mathcal{E}}^{a}_{\nu}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\hskip 2.45836pta}_{\mu\hskip 1.63885pt{\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathcal{E}}^{\circ}_{\nu}-(\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)\right)=\dot{0},

where suffix “
e
” stands for Einstein and
G^{ab}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}
called the Einstein tensor.
We note that these equations are under the vacuum condition, ignoring Lagrangians other than
{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}
.
In reality, the Yang-Mills fields induce the torsion and curvature of space-time.
Later in this report, we will provide concrete expressions of the torsion and the energy-stress tensor induced by the Yang-Mills fields.
Although it is unnecessary for the vacuum equation, we keep factor
1/\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}
in front of equations (
3.2.13a
) and (
3.2.13b
) for future convenience.

3.2.2
Fermions

In the standard theory of particle physics, an electron is represented using the Dirac spinor belonging to the representation
\psi\in{\mathbb{V}}^{~}_{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}\otimes{\mathbb{V}}^{~}_{\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}}
.
On the other hand, there is only a dotted-spinor neutrino in nature, and it is natural to represent it using the Weyl spinor.
This report primarily treats an electron and a neutrino as massless Weyl spinors, ignoring a quark sector in the YMU theory.

The fermion Lagrangian consists of chiral-right and chiral-left parts individually, as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}:={\mathfrak{L}}^{R}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}+{\mathfrak{L}}^{L}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}=\left({\cal L}^{R}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}+{\cal L}^{L}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}\right)\mathfrak{v},

(3.2.14)

since the chiral-right Lagrangian consists of the Weyl spinor and the chiral-left one has a weak pair of dual spinors.
The Lagrangian density must be the Hermitian and
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
invariant scalar.

We start from the chiral-right part, which only has an electron as a fermion.
The chiral-right Lagrangian density consists of undotted spinors as

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{R}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}\!\left(\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~},\bm{\xi}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\right):=\langle\bm{\xi}^{e*}_{~},i{{\not\!d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}\rangle=i\hskip 1.00006pt\bm{\xi}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\left(\hskip-1.99997pt{{\not\!d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}\right)\!,

(3.2.15a)

yielding the equation of motion as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}^{R}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}(\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~},\bm{\xi}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~})}{\delta\bm{\xi}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}}=\dot{0}\implies i{{\not\!d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}=\dot{0}\implies{\gamma}^{\circ}_{~}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\hskip 2.45836pt{\star\star}}_{\circ}\hskip 1.00006pt{S}^{~}_{{\star\star}}\right){\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}=\dot{0},

(3.2.15b)

where we treat the spinor
\bm{\xi}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}
as an independent field of
\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}
.
The equation of motion is called the Weyl equation in physics.
Lagrangian (
3.2.15a
) consists of a bilinear form between the electron Weyl spinors; a bra-part is the complex conjugated spinor, and a ket-part is the spinor with the Dirac operator, which is Hermitian in total.
Since
\bm{\xi}^{*}\in({\mathbb{V}}^{-}={\mathbb{V}}_{\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}})\ni{\not\!d}\bm{\xi}
, the bilinear form is
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
invariant.

For the chiral-left fermion Lagrangian, we utilise the weak pair of dual-spinors (
3.1.17b
) as

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{L}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}\!\left(\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}},\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}\right)

\displaystyle:=\hskip 1.00006pt\left\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{*}_{\textsc{w}},i\bar{{\not\!d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\right\rangle=i\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\bar{{\not\!d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\right)\!,

(3.2.16a)

yielding the equation of motion as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}^{L}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}(\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}},\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}})}{\delta\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies i{\bar{{\not\!d}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}=\overline{\gamma}^{\circ}_{~}\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.99997pt\omega^{\hskip 3.27773pt{\star\star}}_{{\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt\overline{S}^{~}_{{\star\star}}\right)+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}\bm{\tau}^{~}_{I}\right)\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}=\dot{0}.

(3.2.16b)

The spinor-gauge covariant differential acts on the weak pair of dual-spinors as (
3.1.35
).
The chiral-left fermion Lagrangian provides an electron-neutrino weak-interaction after quantisation.
The chiral-left spinor Lagrangian is functional concerning the Yang–Mills gauge field, too; thus, we have the additional equation of motions such that

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}^{L}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})}{\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}=\dot{0}

\displaystyle\implies-c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{GgG})}}}{{=}}-c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\left(\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu}_{~}+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}\right)\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}=\dot{0}.

(3.2.17)

The right-hand side of (
3.2.17
) appears as a source term of the Yang–Mills gauge boson such that

\displaystyle\text{(\ref{YMGBEoM})}|

\displaystyle\implies\hat{d}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}=c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.99997pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\mathfrak{V}_{\circ},\implies\eta^{a{\circ}}\left[\text{l.h.s. of (\ref{YMGBEoM3})}\right]^{~}_{\circ}=c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.99997pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{\textsc{w}}\gamma^{a}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}.

(3.2.18)

3.2.3
Higgs spinor

The standard form of the scalar Lagrangian consists of a differentiated scalar field squared.
We introduce the Higgs Lagrangian as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}):=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{kin}_{\textsc{h}}({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}})-V\left({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}\right)\mathfrak{v},

(3.2.19a)

where
V({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}){\in}\Omega^{0}({T^{*}\hskip-1.99997pt{\cal M}}){\otimes}{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{e}}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}{\otimes}Ad({\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{u}}(2))
and

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{kin}_{\textsc{h}}({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}):=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}){{\circ}}(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\langle{\bm{\phi}}^{*}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}\rangle\mathfrak{v}=\frac{1}{2}(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})\langle{\bm{\phi}}^{*}_{\textsc{h}},i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}\rangle\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\phi}}^{*}_{\textsc{h}},\left(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}\rangle\mathfrak{v}={\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}\cdot\left(\left(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}\right)\mathfrak{v},

(3.2.19b)

yielding the equation of motion as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathscr{I}_{\textsc{h}}({\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\dagger})}{\delta{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=\dot{0}\implies\left(\left(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}-\frac{\delta V({\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\dagger})}{\delta{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}}\right){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}=\dot{0}.

(3.2.19c)

The Dirac operator squared is called the Lichnerowicz formula
[
27
,
26
]
.
The
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
symmetry is internal one in the Lorentzian YMU theory; thus, the Higgs spinor does not directly couple to the spin connection.
In this case, the Dirac operator squared can be written as

\displaystyle-\left(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta+\widehat{\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt},

(3.2.20)

where
\widehat{\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
is a differential operator defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt

\displaystyle:=\iota_{\gamma}\iota_{\gamma}(d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}).

(3.2.21)

Formula (
3.2.20
) is called the Lichnerowicz formula in a flat space-time, which is provided by direct calculations as

\displaystyle(\ref{Lf1})

\displaystyle=\iota_{\gamma}\iota_{\gamma}\left(\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006ptd-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)\wedge\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006ptd-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)\right),

\displaystyle=-i{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left(\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\not\!\partial+\left(\hskip-1.99997pt\not\!\partial\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip-1.00006pt\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip-1.00006pt\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right),

\displaystyle=-(i\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\not\!\partial^{~}}+\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}})\left(i\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip-1.99997pt\not\!\partial^{~}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip-1.99997pt\not\!{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\right)-\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta=-\left(i{{\not\!d}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}-\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta,

(3.2.22)

\displaystyle\implies(\ref{Lf0}).

The Lichnerowicz formula gives a representation of the Laplacian operator using the covariant differential and the double contraction as

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta

\displaystyle=(\iota_{\gamma}d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})(\iota_{\gamma}d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})-\iota_{\gamma}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\gamma}(d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}\hskip 1.00006ptd^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}).

(3.2.23)

Owing to the relation

\displaystyle\left({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}d+d{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}d{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}+d({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}})={\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}d{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}+d({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}-{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}d{\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}=(d{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},

the curvature operator can be written from the second line of (
3.2.22
) as

\displaystyle\widehat{\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}

\displaystyle=-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\gamma}\iota_{\gamma}\left((d{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\wedge}{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)=-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt},~~\text{where}~~\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}:=\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{F}_{\circ\circ}^{I}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\tau_{I}.

Consequently, we obtain the Lagrangian and the equation of motion as

(
3.2.19a
)

\displaystyle\implies{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}({\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}})=-{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta+i{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}-V({\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}),

(3.2.24a)

(
3.2.19c
)

\displaystyle\implies\left(\bm{1}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\Delta+i{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}+\frac{\delta V({\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}},{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{h}}^{\dagger})}{\delta{\bm{\phi}}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}}\right){\bm{\phi}}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}=\dot{0}.

(3.2.24b)

The curvature contracted with the Clifford algebra
\not\!{\hskip-3.00003pt\not\!\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
is called the
twisting curvature of the Clifford module
[
26
]

3.2.4
Fermion mass term

Fermionic matter Lagrangian (
3.2.14
) provides an equation of motion for massless electrons and neutrinos and their interaction.
In reality, an electron has a finite mass; thus, we introduce the mass term into the YMU Lagrangian.
We discuss two methods: a direct method and a spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) method.

a) direct method:

A Hermitian term

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{m_{e}}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}

\displaystyle=m_{e}\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{~},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}\rangle=m_{e}\hskip 1.99997pt({\dot{\bm{\xi}}}^{e}_{~})^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}\cdot\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}=m_{e}\hskip 1.99997pt[\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}]^{~}_{A}\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon_{2}^{\hskip 2.45836ptAB}\left[\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}\right]^{~}_{B}\!,

(3.2.25)

provides an electron mass term of the fermion Lagrangian.
We note that
\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}
; thus it has undotted index.
Since both a bra-part and a ket-part belong to the
{\mathbb{V}}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}
space, bi-linear form (
3.2.25
) is
{{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}}
invariant.
When we utilise the chiral basis for an electron spinor as

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}^{e}_{~}=\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}+\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{d}},

\displaystyle~~\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}=\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}+\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{\textsc{d}},

(3.2.26a)

we obtain the electron mass term as

\displaystyle\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{u}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}\rangle=\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{d}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{d}}\rangle=0

\displaystyle\implies{\cal L}^{m_{e}}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}=-m_{e}\left(\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{u}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{d}}\rangle-\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{d}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}\rangle\right)\!.

(3.2.26b)

The mass term consists of the bilinear form between chiral-left and chiral-right spinors, at the same time, chiral-left and chiral-right spinors, contrasting to the fermion Lagrangian (
3.2.14
).

b) SSB method:

The standard YMU Lagrangian has the interaction term between Higgs and Weyl spinors, which is cast into the fermion-mass term after the spontaneous symmetry breaking.
This interaction term keeps local
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
and internal
SU^{~}_{\hskip-1.22911pt\textsc{w}}\hskip-1.00006pt(2)
symmetries.
This report describes the fermion mass term owing to the spontaneous symmetry breaking using the weak-pair of dual-spinors.

We introduce a Higgs-fermion bilinear form using dual-spinors as

\displaystyle\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{w}}^{~}}\rangle\overset{(\ref{XXxXpX})}{=}\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{w}}^{\textsc{u}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{w}}^{\textsc{u}}}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu}_{~}+\langle{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{w}}^{\textsc{d}}},{\hat{\bm{\phi}}_{\textsc{w}}^{\textsc{d}}}\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}=\phi^{~}_{\textsc{u}}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu}_{~}+\phi^{~}_{\textsc{d}}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}.

(3.2.27)

Then, we provide the fermion-mass Lagrangian as

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{m_{e}}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\langle\langle\dot{\bm{\Xi}}^{*}_{\textsc{w}},{\bm{\phi}_{\textsc{w}}^{~}}\rangle,{\bm{\Xi}}^{~}_{\textsc{w}}\rangle=\lambda\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{~}_{\textsc{u}}\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu*}_{~},{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu}_{~}\rangle+\lambda\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{~}_{\textsc{d}}\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{~},{\bm{\xi}}^{e}_{~}\rangle,

\displaystyle=-\lambda\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{~}_{\textsc{d}}\left(\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{u}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{d}}\rangle-\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{d}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}\rangle\right)-\lambda\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{~}_{\textsc{u}}\left(\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu*}_{\textsc{u}},\bm{\xi}^{\nu}_{\textsc{d}}\rangle-\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{\nu*}_{\textsc{d}},\bm{\xi}^{\nu}_{\textsc{u}}\rangle\right)\!.

(3.2.28)

When we set the Higgs spinor as

\displaystyle\left(\phi^{~}_{\textsc{u}}=0,~\phi^{~}_{\textsc{d}}=v_{0}/\sqrt{2}\in\mathbb{R},~m_{e}=\lambda\hskip 1.00006ptv_{0}/\sqrt{2}\right)\implies(\ref{me2})=(\ref{me1}),

(3.2.29)

where
v_{0}
is a vacuum expectation value, we have a finite electron mass proportional to the vacuum expectation value.

When the neutrino is massless, the above Lagrangian is enough to describe a leptonic part of the standard theory of particle physics since the chiral-right singlet neutrino does not interact with any other fields than the graviton.
On the other hand, in the quark sector, we know that the up-type quarks also have a mass.
This report does not treat quarks in the YMU theory.

3.3
Torsion and stress-energy forms

In
section
3.2.1
, we presented two equations of motion for the gravitational field: the torsion equation and the Einstein equation.
A static point of the action integral concerning the vierbein provides the torsion equation, and that concerning the spin-connection provides the Einstein equation.
Other equations of motion are obtained as the static point concerning matter, Higgs and Weyl-spinor fields.
In reality, the fermion Lagrangian has the spin-connection and all Lagrangians have the vierbein; thus, the static point concerning the vierbein and spin-connection contributes to the torsion and Einstein equations.
We introduce the torsion and stress-energy forms in the YMU theory.

Torsion two-form
{\mathfrak{T}}^{~}_{\bullet}
and stress-energy three-form
\mathfrak{E}^{~}_{\bullet}
of the YMU Lagrangian for
\bullet\in\{\textsc{y\hskip-1.00006ptm\hskip-1.00006ptu},\textsc{y\hskip-1.00006ptm},\textsc{g}\hskip-0.50003pt\textsc{r},\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{m}},\textsc{h},\textsc{m}\text{-}\textsc{h}\}
are respectively defined as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{ab{\circ\circ}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet}^{\hskip 1.63885pt{\circ}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}_{~}:=\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\bullet}}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}}-d\left(\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\bullet}}{\delta(d{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab})}\right),~~\mathfrak{E}_{\hskip 1.63885pta}^{\bullet}:=\hbar\left(\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\bullet}}{\delta{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}}-d\left(\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}^{~}_{\bullet}}{\delta(d{\mathfrak{e}}^{a})}\right)\right)\!.

(3.3.1)

We note that the above definition has opposite sign to that of the Einstein form and torsion form.
These form objects have physical dimensions as

\displaystyle\left[{\mathfrak{T}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet}\right]=L^{-1}~~\mathrm{and}~~\left[\mathfrak{E}_{~}^{\bullet}\right]=E,

and their tensor coefficients are denoted as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet}^{\hskip 1.63885pta}=:\frac{1}{2}T_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet\hskip 2.45836pt{\circ\circ}}^{\hskip 1.63885pta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}~~\text{and}~~\mathfrak{E}_{\hskip 1.63885pta}^{\bullet}=:E^{\bullet}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{V}_{a};

thus, these tensors have physical dimensions as

\displaystyle\left[T_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet}\right]=L^{-1}/L^{2}~~\text{and}~~\left[E^{\bullet}\right]=E/L^{3}.

Tensor coefficient
T_{\hskip-0.81949pt\bullet}
is a torsion density per unit two-dimensional surface, and
E^{\bullet}
is an energy-stress density per unit three-volume.

a) torsion two-form:

First, we calculate a torsion induced by the fermion Lagrangian.
We denote a variation of the Lagrangian concerning the spin-connection as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{ab{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{T}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}^{\hskip 2.45836pt{\circ}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}_{~}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}}={c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\left(\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\gamma_{~}^{\circ}{{S}}^{~}_{ab}\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{\xi}\right)\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}.

(3.3.2)

The torsion form can be obtained by solving the algebraic equation (
3.3.2
) as

\displaystyle\text{(\ref{kform})}\implies T_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}\hskip 2.45836ptbc}^{\hskip 1.63885pta}=\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\gamma_{~}^{a}{{S}}^{~}_{bc}+2\delta^{a}_{\hskip 1.63885pt[b}{\eta}^{~}_{c]{\circ}}\gamma_{~}^{\circ}\right)\hskip-1.99997pt\bm{\xi}\implies{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}=\frac{1}{2}T_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}\hskip 2.45836pt{\circ\circ}}^{\hskip 1.63885pta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}.

(3.3.3)

Since only the fermion Lagrangian includes the spin connection, this is all of the non-zero torsion forms in the YMU theory.

b) stress-energy three-form:

In contrast with the spin-connection, all sub-Lagrangians in the YMU Lagrangian include the vierbein form as a part of the volume form.
We can construct the stress-energy three-form commonly from the Lagrangian of a type

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{v})=\Phi_{\bullet}(f)\mathfrak{v}\quad\text{as}\quad\mathfrak{E}_{a}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\hbar\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{v})}{\delta{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}_{~}}=\hbar\Phi_{\bullet}(f)\frac{\delta~}{\delta{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}_{~}}\left(\frac{1}{4!}\epsilon_{{\circ\circ}{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\right),

\displaystyle=\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\Phi_{\bullet}(f)\frac{\hbar}{3!}\epsilon_{a{\circ}{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}=\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\Phi_{\bullet}(f)\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{V}^{~}_{a},

\displaystyle\implies E^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\Phi_{\bullet}(f),

where
\Phi(f)
is a scalar functional of physical fields
f
.
Consequently, the stress-energy form owing to the Yang–Mills field and the fermion Lagrangians is provided as

\displaystyle(\ref{YMLL})

\displaystyle\implies E^{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}=-\frac{\hbar}{4}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ},

(3.3.4a)

\displaystyle(\ref{WspinEoML})

\displaystyle\implies E^{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}=\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}_{~}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{~}^{\circ}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt\omega_{\circ}^{~{\star\star}}{{S}}^{~}_{\star\star}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}\tau^{~}_{I}\right)\!\bm{\xi}

(3.3.4b)

\displaystyle(\ref{LHiggs2})

\displaystyle\implies E^{\textsc{h}}_{a}=\hbar\left(\eta^{\circ\circ}(\partial^{~}_{\circ}\bm{\phi}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}})(\partial^{~}_{\circ}\bm{\phi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}})-i\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{F}_{\circ\circ}^{I}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\gamma^{\circ}_{~}\tau_{I}(\bm{\phi}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}}\bm{\phi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}})-V(\bm{\phi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},\bm{\phi}^{\dagger}_{\textsc{h}})\right)

(3.3.4c)

\displaystyle(\ref{me1})

\displaystyle\implies E^{m_{e}}=-\hbar\hskip 1.00006ptm_{e}\left(\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{u}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{d}}\rangle-\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}^{e*}_{\textsc{d}},\bm{\xi}^{e}_{\textsc{u}}\rangle\right).

(3.3.4d)

In (
3.3.4c
), we use integration by parts.

In summary, the Einstein equation and the torsion equation in the YMU theory are respectively provided as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{a}^{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{E}^{\textsc{y\hskip-0.70004ptm}}_{a}+\mathfrak{E}^{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}}_{a}+\mathfrak{E}^{\textsc{h}}_{a}+\mathfrak{E}^{m_{e}}_{a}~~\text{and}~~{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}={\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{\textsc{f}_{\hskip-0.73616pt\textsc{m}}},

(3.3.5a)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{a}^{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\mathfrak{G}_{a}=\frac{1}{2\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}\epsilon_{a{\circ}{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ\circ}}\hskip-1.99997pt\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\hskip 0.81949pt{\circ}}~~\text{and}~~{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{g}\hskip-0.40974pt\textsc{r}}:=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}d_{\mathfrak{w}}{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}.

(3.3.5b)

This section constructs the YMU theory in the inertial
Lorentzian
manifold based on mathematical tools prepared up to the previous section.
Thus, we omit the subscript “
l
” for simplicity.
The YMU theory treated in this study is the gauge theory with internal
{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptU\hskip-1.00006pt}(2)
symmetry and local Lorentz symmetry.
We formulate the theory in the inertial manifold; after fixing the frame and treating the theory to the local inertial frame, the global
GL(4)
symmetry is broken while keeping the local
{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptO\hskip-1.00006pt}(1,3)
symmetry.
The local information of the global manifold is encapsulated in the spin-connection form
{\mathfrak{w}}
in this formalism.

4
Amphometric space

We construct the YMU theory in the Lorentzian metric space in the previous section.
This section develops a method to simultaneously treat Euclidean and Lorentzian metrics in the YMU theory.
In the quantum field theory, we utilise an analytic continuation concerning the time coordinate, known as the Wick rotation.
A primary purpose of the Wick rotation is to improve integration convergence.
Concomitantly, it determines analyticity of a particle propagator.
The analytic continuation is the complexification of the time coordinate with a pure imaginary number, and a point in Minkowski space is mapped to that in Euclidean space.
We propose to refine the method: two metric spaces are continuously connected using a one-parameter family of metrics, namely the
amphometric
, which induces a one-parameter subgroup of
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
.
Though the Wick rotation is a technical issue when performing integration in the Minkowski space, the amphometric method provides a deep understanding of the physical relation between Euclidean and Lorentzian metrics.

4.1
Amphometric

This section proposes a refinement of the Wick-rotation to simultaneously treat Euclidean and Lorentzian metric spaces as two boundaries of the one-parameter subgroup in
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
.
In
Section
2.4
, we introduced a Clifford algebra in both Euclidean and Lorentzian metric spaces and defined the chiral operator to be shared in both spaces.
Moreover, we introduced the maps (
2.4.15
) and (
2.4.25
), transforming the quaternion representation to the representation of dimension-4 in Euclidean and Lorentzian metric spaces.
We unify these maps to a single map using the smooth function
\kappa(\theta)\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{c}(\ref{ijk2E})\\
(\ref{ijk2L})\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Sigma^{~}_{\theta}:Sp(1){\rightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}{G\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}:\{1,i,j,k\}^{t}\mapsto\bm{\sigma}^{~}_{\theta}:=\{\bm{1}^{~}_{2},\kappa(\theta)\bm{\sigma}\}^{t}\!.

When we require it to behave
\kappa(\theta=0)=-i
and
\kappa(\theta=\pm 1)=\pm 1
, it provides dimension-2 representation of the Euclidean Clifford algebra at
\theta=0
and the Lorentzian Clifford algebra at
\theta=\pm 1
.
Moreover, we require
\kappa(\theta)
to be a non-zero function in
\theta\in[-1,1]
.
We propose the following function for
\kappa(\theta)
:

\displaystyle\kappa(\theta)

\displaystyle:=e^{i\pi(\theta-1)/2}=\begin{cases}+1&\theta=+1\\
-i&\theta=\hskip 8.00003pt0\\
-1&\theta=-1\end{cases}\!.

(4.1.3)

Figure
1
depicts a behaviour of
\kappa(\theta)
in
\theta\in[-1,1]
.

Figure 1:

\kappa(\theta)
is drawn in
-1\leq\theta\leq 1
.
Solid- and dashed-lines show real- and imaginary-parts of the function, respectively.

Suppose
{\cal M}^{~}_{\theta}
is a four-dimensional smooth manifold equipping the Clifford algebra concerning the symmetric
({4\hskip-1.99997pt\times\hskip-1.99997pt4})
complex matrix
{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}
.
The map (
2.6.2
) with
\bm{\sigma}^{~}_{\theta}
induces the representation of dimension-2 for real vector
{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{v}\theta}=(v^{0},v^{1},v^{2},v^{3})^{t}{\hskip 1.00006pt\in\hskip 1.00006pt}V({T\hskip-1.99997pt{\cal M}}^{~}_{\theta}:\mathbb{R})
as

\displaystyle\Sigma^{~}_{\theta}:V(\mathbb{R}^{4}){\rightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}V({S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}):{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{v}\theta}\mapsto{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}:=\bm{\sigma}^{t}_{\theta}\cdot{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{v}\theta}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}v^{0}+\kappa(\theta)v^{3}&\kappa(\theta)\left(v^{1}-iv^{2}\right)\\
\kappa(\theta)\left(v^{1}+iv^{2}\right)&v^{0}-\kappa(\theta)v^{3}\\
\end{array}\right)\!.

We refer to a complex (
{2\hskip-1.99997pt\times\hskip-1.99997pt2}
)-matrix of the shape
(\ref{SigmathetaV})
as the
apmphovector
, which is a unitary matrix at
\theta=0
and the Hermitian matrix at
\theta=\pm 1
.
We determine the complex-valued symmetric tensor
{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}
owing to the relation

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{u}}_{\textsc{s}\theta},{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\rangle

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left({\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}[{\bm{u}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}]\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}[{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}]-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}[{\bm{u}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}]\right)={\bm{u}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}^{t}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}=u^{0}v^{0}+e^{i\pi\theta}\left(u^{1}v^{1}+u^{2}v^{2}+u^{3}v^{3}\right)\!,

(4.1.6a)

which provides

\displaystyle(\ref{<vu>Q})

\displaystyle\implies{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}=\text{diag}[1,e^{i\pi\theta},e^{i\pi\theta},e^{i\pi\theta}],~~\text{yielding}~~{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}=\begin{cases}{{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\textsc{e}}}},&\theta=\hskip 8.00003pt0\\
{{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\textsc{l}}}},&\theta=\pm 1\end{cases}\!.

(4.1.6b)

Complex matrix
{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}}
is the Lorentzian metric at both ends of the domain
\theta\in[-1,1]
; thus, we call it as the
amphometric tensor
, and the pair
({\cal M}^{~}_{\theta},{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}})
is referred to as the
amphometric space
in this report.
We define the
amphonorm
of the vector
{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}
as

\displaystyle\|{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\|^{2}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta},v_{\textsc{s}\theta}\rangle=\text{det}\left[{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\right]=({v}^{0})^{2}+e^{i\pi\theta}\left(({v}^{1})^{2}+({v}^{2})^{2}+({v}^{3})^{2}\right)\!.

(4.1.6c)

Consider four-dimensional
rotation
operator
\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}
whose
({4\hskip-1.99997pt\times\hskip-1.99997pt4})
-matric representation
\bm{\Lambda}_{\theta}
fulfiling

\displaystyle\text{det}[\bm{\Lambda}_{\theta}]=1,~~\bm{\Lambda}_{\theta=1}\in{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptO\hskip-1.00006pt}(1,3)~\text{and}~\bm{\Lambda}_{\theta=0}\in{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptO\hskip-1.00006pt}(4).

A set of
\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}
is denoted
Ampho(2)
.
The the representation of dimension-2 relates with the representation of dimension-4 as

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{v}\theta}=\bm{\Lambda}_{\theta}\cdot{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}^{~}\leftrightarrow\bm{\sigma}^{t}_{\theta}\cdot{\bm{v}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{v}\theta}={\bm{v}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{v}\theta}=\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}\cdot{\bm{v}}^{~}_{\textsc{v}\theta}\cdot\bm{g}_{\theta}^{\dagger}\implies\text{det}[{\bm{v}}^{\prime}_{\bullet\theta}]=\text{det}[{\bm{v}}^{~}_{\bullet\theta}],~\bullet\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{s}\}\!.

(4.1.7)

Thus,
Ampho(2)
is a one-parameter subgroup of
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
.

The amphometric connects the space-time symmetries of
SO(4)
and
SO(1,3)
groups, forming the one-parameter subgroup in
{G\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
.
The amphometric space
({\cal M}^{~}_{\theta},{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\theta}})
provides the Euclidean space
({{{\cal M}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}}},{{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\textsc{e}}}})
at
\theta=0
and the Minkowski space
({{{\cal M}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}}}},{{\bm{\eta}^{~}_{\textsc{l}}}})
at
\theta=\pm 1
.
On the other hand, it is not the Riemannian manifold at points other than
\theta=0,\pm 1
.

4.2
Causal subgroup and invariant bilinear form

When we allow all
{G\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
group actions on
Ampho(2)
at
\theta\neq\pm 1
, they violate the causality at
\theta=\pm 1
.
This section introduces a subgroup of
Ampho(2)
, namely the causal subgroup, preserving the causality of the physical phenomenon in the Lorentzian metric space.
The restriction of group actions in the causal subgroup corresponds to choosing one axis as the “time” axis and prohibiting mixing the “time” direction with the “spatial” directions in Euclidean space.

Although the Euclidean manifold is the simply connected space owing to the
SO(4)
transformation (four-dimensional rotation), the Minkowski manifold has four disconnected subspaces under the Lorentz transformation: the null-distance region, the space-like region, the time-like past region, and the time-like future region.
When we mix
SO(1,3)
and
SO(4)
symmetries, time-like separated two points in the Minkowski space can be mapped to the space-like separation or reversed time-ordered position by the
SO(4)
rotation and it violates the causality between those two points.
We restrict transformations in the amphometric space to preserve the causality for physical theories.

Definition 4.1

(Causal subgroup)
.

Suppose matrix
\bm{g}^{~}_{c}(\theta){\hskip 1.00006pt\in\hskip 1.00006pt}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2){\hskip 1.00006pt\subset\hskip 1.00006pt}Ampho(2)
fulfils

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{~}_{c}(\theta=\pm 1)\in{\hskip-1.00006ptS\hskip-1.00006ptO\hskip-1.00006pt}^{\uparrow}\hskip-1.00006pt(1,3){\subset}SO(1,3)~~\text{and}~~\bm{g}^{~}_{c}(\theta=0){\hskip 1.00006pt\in\hskip 1.00006pt}SO_{~}^{\perp}\hskip-1.00006pt(3){\subset}SO(4),

(4.2.1)

which acts on the amphovector as

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc}:End\left({C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right):{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\mapsto{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}^{\prime}:=\tau\left(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc}\right)\left({\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\right).

(4.2.2)

The
\mathrm{({4\hskip-1.99997pt\times\hskip-1.99997pt4})}
-matrix groups in
(
4.2.1
)
mean the corresponding
\mathrm{({2\hskip-1.99997pt\times\hskip-1.99997pt2})}
-matrix ones shown
(
4.1.7
)
.
We call
\bm{g}^{~}_{\theta}
as the causal operator and
{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)
as the causal subgroup of
Ampho(2)
.

We consider the following
{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)
group actions in the amphometric space:

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{a}_{c}(\theta;\varphi,\chi)

\displaystyle:=\left.\bm{g}^{a}_{{r}}(\varphi){\circ}\hskip 1.00006pt\bm{g}^{a}_{b}(\chi)\right|^{~}_{\bm{\sigma}\rightarrow(\chi\hskip 1.63885pt\theta-i\varphi)\bm{\sigma}}=\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left(-i\sigma^{a}\frac{\varphi}{2}\right)}\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\sigma^{a}\frac{\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta}{2}\right)},

(4.2.3a)

which acts on an amphovector as

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}^{\prime}_{\textsc{s}\theta}

\displaystyle=\tau\left(\bm{g}^{a}_{c}(\theta;\varphi,\chi)\right)\left({\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\theta}\right)=\Sigma^{~}_{\theta}\begin{cases}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}v^{0}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{1}_{\theta}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\\
v^{0}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{1}_{\theta}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\\
v^{2}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}-v^{3}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}\\
v^{2}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}+v^{3}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}\\
\end{array}\right)&a=1,\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}v^{0}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{2}_{\theta}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\\
v^{1}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}+v^{3}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}\\
v^{0}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{2}_{\theta}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\\
-v^{1}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}+v^{3}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}\\
\end{array}\right)&a=2,\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}v^{0}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{3}_{\theta}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\\
v^{1}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}-v^{2}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}\\
v^{1}_{\theta}\sin{(\varphi)}+v^{2}_{\theta}\cos{(\varphi)}\\
v^{0}\sinh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)+v^{3}_{\theta}\cosh(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)\end{array}\right)&a=3,\end{cases}

(4.2.3b)

where

\displaystyle\left(v^{0},v^{1}_{\theta},v^{2}_{\theta},v^{3}_{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle:=\left(v^{0},\kappa(\theta)v^{1},\kappa(\theta)v^{2},\kappa(\theta)v^{3}\right).

(4.2.3c)

This action simultaneously induces the rotation around the
v^{a}
-axis in the three-dimensional sub-manifold perpendicular to the
v^{0}
-axis and the
amphoboost
along the
v^{a}
-axis, where the amphoboost is the true boost only at
\theta\hskip-1.99997pt=\hskip-1.99997pt\pm 1
.
We can confirm that the determinant of the action (
4.2.3a
) is unity as

\displaystyle\text{det}\left[\bm{g}^{a}_{c}(\theta;\varphi,\chi)\right]

\displaystyle=\text{det}\left[\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left(-i\sigma^{a}\frac{\varphi}{2}\right)}\right]\text{det}\left[\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\sigma^{a}\frac{\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta}{2}\right)}\right]=\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left({\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[-i\sigma^{a}\frac{\varphi}{2}\right]\right)}\exp{\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\hskip-1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\sigma^{a}\frac{\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta}{2}\right]\right)}=1,

since the Pauli matrices are traceless.
Consequently, (
4.2.3a
) preserves bilinear form (
4.1.6a
).
The causal operator keeps causality by preserving the sign of the
0
’th component of amphovectors.
The three-dimensional rotation
\bm{g}^{a}_{{r}}(\varphi)
and boost
\bm{g}^{a}_{b}(\chi\hskip 1.99997pt\theta)
are endomorphisms on
SO^{\uparrow}\hskip-1.00006pt(1,3)
and
SO^{\perp}(3)
; thus, (
4.2.3a
) is the causal operator.
Moreover, the complex conjugate of the causal operator is given as

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{a}_{c}(\theta;\varphi,\chi)^{*}

\displaystyle=\bm{g}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{r}}^{a=2}(\pi)\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{g}^{a}_{c}(-\theta;\varphi,\chi)\hskip 1.99997pt\bm{g}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{r}}^{a=2}(\pi)^{\dagger}~~\text{for}~~a\in\{1,2,3\}.

(4.2.4)

Thus, two irreducible representations
\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}
and
\bm{g}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}^{*}
in
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
are homotopy equivalence in the amphometric space embedding
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
at both ends.
Therefore,
{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)
has a unique irreducible representation.
The general causal operator can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc}

\displaystyle=\bm{g}^{a_{1}}_{c}(\theta;\varphi^{~}_{1},\chi^{~}_{1})\cdots\bm{g}^{a_{n}}_{c}(\theta;\varphi^{~}_{n},\chi^{~}_{n}).

(4.2.5)

The amphovector has the invariant bilinear form under the causal subgroup at any values of
\theta
.
We schematically show a relation between the Euclidean and the Lorentzian vectors in the amphometric space as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\theta=-1&{\longleftrightarrow}&\theta=0&{\longleftrightarrow}&\theta=1\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}^{-}&{\longleftrightarrow}&{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{e}}&{\longleftrightarrow}&{\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}^{+}\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
\tau\left(\bm{g}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}}^{*}\right)\left({\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{l}}^{-}\right)&{\longleftrightarrow}&\tau\left(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}}\right)\left({\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{e}}\right)&{\longleftrightarrow}&\tau\left(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}}\right)\left({\bm{v}}_{\textsc{s}\hskip-0.49164pt\textsc{e}}^{+}\right)\end{array}\!.

The irreducible representation of the amphospinor is given similarly to (
2.3.11
):
In the amphometric space,
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
in (
2.3.3
) are not on
S_{3}
in general, but are written using
(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2}){\in}(S^{3}=SU(2))\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}
as

\displaystyle z_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{1}]+\kappa(\theta)\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{1}],~~z_{2}=-i\kappa(\theta)(\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{2}]+i\hskip 1.00006pt\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{2}]),~~\text{where}~~\left|\tilde{z}_{1}\right|^{2}+\left|\tilde{z}_{2}\right|^{2}=1.

(4.2.11)

The polynomial defined in (
2.3.4
) with the amphometric and its vector space are denoted as
\lambda_{s}^{\theta}
and
{\mathbb{V}}^{\theta}_{\hskip-0.81949pts}
, respectively.
If we consider the definition (
4.2.11
) to be a transformation from
\{\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2}\}
to
\{z_{1},z_{2}\}
, it is a linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\text{Amp}:SU(2)\otimes[-1,1]{\rightarrow}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2):(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})\otimes\theta{\mapsto}\text{Amp}(\theta)(\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2})=(\ref{amphoq})~{\in\hskip 3.99994pt}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2){\subset}Ampho(2).

(4.2.12)

This transformation provides the representation for
{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)
from the
SU(2)
representation given by (
2.3.1
) as

\displaystyle\text{Amp}(\theta)\left(\left.(\ref{gEz1z2})\right|^{~}_{z_{i}\rightarrow\tilde{z}_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\left(\pi_{\hskip-0.81949pts}^{\theta}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}){\circ}\lambda_{s}^{\theta}\right)(z_{1},z_{2})=\lambda_{s}^{\theta}(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2}),~~~\text{with}~~~\left(\begin{array}[]{c}z^{\prime}_{1}\\
z^{\prime}_{2}\end{array}\right)=\bm{g}^{-1}_{\theta}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}z^{~}_{1}\\
z^{~}_{2}\end{array}\right).

This is one-to-one transformation and
\pi_{\hskip-0.81949pts}
is irreducible; thus. the representation
\pi_{\hskip-0.81949pts}^{\theta}
is also irreducible.
We obtain group and Lie-algebra representation as

\displaystyle\pi^{\theta}_{s}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc})

\displaystyle=\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc},~~\text{and}~~\dot{\pi}^{\theta}_{s}(\mathfrak{g}_{c})=\mathfrak{g}_{c}\in{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2),

(4.2.17a)

yielding

\displaystyle\pi^{\theta}_{s}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc})\big|^{~}_{\theta=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{e}},~~\pi^{\theta}_{s}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc})\big|^{~}_{\theta=1}=\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}},~~\text{and}~~\pi^{\theta}_{s}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc})\big|^{~}_{\theta=-1}=\pi^{~}_{\bar{s}}(\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{l}})=\bm{g}_{\textsc{l}}^{*},

(4.2.17b)

where
{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}|\theta(2)
is a space of the Lie-algebra for the causal subgroup.

Next, we consider two Lie-group homomorphisms (
2.3.1a
) and (
2.3.1b
) under the causal subgroup.
The causal subgroup
{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)
at
\theta=0
is a subgroup of
SU(2)
, and that at
\theta=1
is equivalent to
{S\hskip-1.00006ptL(2,\mathbb{C})}
, respectively.
At
(-1,1)\supset\theta\neq 0
, we obtain the Lie-algebra
{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\subset\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(2,\mathbb{C})
as (
4.2.17a
).
We schematically summarize the relations among them as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}(\ref{ismorSO4})&\longrightarrow&{\mathfrak{s}}\mathfrak{o}(4)&=&{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{u}}(2)&\oplus&{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{u}}(2)\\
&&&&\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\subset$}&&\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\subset$}\\
&&&&\left.{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\right|^{~}_{\theta=0}&\oplus&\left.{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\right|^{~}_{\theta=0}\\
&&&&\updownarrow&&\updownarrow\\
&&{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\otimes[-1,1]&=&{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\otimes[0,1]&\oplus&{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\otimes[-1,0]\\
&&&&\updownarrow&&\updownarrow\\
&&&&\left.{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\right|^{~}_{\theta=1}&\oplus&~\left.{{\mathfrak{C}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{u}}{\mathfrak{s}}}_{\theta}(2)\right|^{~}_{\theta=-1}\\
&&&&\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{=}&&\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{=}\\
(\ref{ismorSO13})&\longrightarrow&{\mathfrak{s}}\mathfrak{o}(1,3)\otimes\mathbb{C}&=&{\mathfrak{s}}\mathfrak{l}(2,\mathbb{C})&\oplus&\overline{{\mathfrak{s}}\mathfrak{l}(2,\mathbb{C})}\end{array}.

We introduced the tensor product of two vector spaces in the amphometric space with the internal (non space-time) group
G
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textsc{s}}(\theta^{~}_{1},\theta^{~}_{2};G)

\displaystyle:={\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta_{1}}\left[\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc}{\in}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right]\otimes_{G}{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta_{2}}\left[\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptc}{\in}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right],

(4.2.27)

where
{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta}\left[\bm{g}{\in}G\right]
means the vector space
{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\lambda}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
having the invariant bilinear form under the group action
\bm{g}
of the group
G
in the amphometric space.
This representation gives the Euclidean one (
2.3.11
) at
\theta=0
and the Lorentzian one (
2.3.13
) at
\theta=1
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta}\left[\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}{\in}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right]\big|_{\theta=0}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\textsc{e}},~~~{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta}\left[\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}{\in}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right]\big|_{\theta=1}={\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\textsc{l}},~~\text{and}~~{\mathbb{V}}_{\!{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}^{\theta}\left[\bm{g}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}{\in}{C\hskip-1.00006ptaus}(2)\right]\big|_{\theta=-1}={\mathbb{V}}_{\!\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}}^{\textsc{l}}.

A bilinear form for spinors in the amphometric space (amphospinor)
\bm{\xi}(\theta),\bm{\zeta}(\theta){\hskip 1.00006pt\in\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathbb{V}}^{\theta}_{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}
is defined by combining (
2.5.8
) and (
2.5.13
) as

\displaystyle\langle\bm{\xi}(\theta),\bm{\zeta}(\theta)\rangle

\displaystyle=\left[\xi(-\theta)\right]^{*}_{A}\left[\bm{\epsilon}(\theta)\right]^{AB}\left[\bm{\zeta}(\theta)\right]_{B}~~\text{where}~\bm{\epsilon}(\theta):=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{rr}\cos(\pi\theta/2)&\sin(\pi\theta/2)\\
-\sin(\pi\theta/2)&\cos(\pi\theta/2)\end{array}\!\right)\!,

yielding

\displaystyle\bm{\epsilon}(0)=\bm{1}^{~}_{2}\hskip 6.99997pt

\displaystyle\implies\langle\bm{\xi}(0),\bm{\zeta}(0)\rangle=\langle\bm{\phi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}},\bm{\varphi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}\rangle,

(4.2.28c)

where
\bm{\phi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}
and
\bm{\varphi}^{~}_{\textsc{h}}
are Higgs spinors corresponding to
\bm{\xi}(\theta=0)
and
\bm{\zeta}(\theta=0)
, respectively.
We obtain that

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}(\theta)\big|_{\theta=+1}\in{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}\iff\bm{\xi}^{*}(-\theta)\big|_{\theta=+1}\in{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}},

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}(\theta)\big|_{\theta=-1}\in{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}}\iff\bm{\xi}^{*}(-\theta)\big|_{\theta=-1}\in{\mathbb{V}}^{\textsc{l}}_{\overline{{1\hskip-0.81949pt/\hskip-0.40974pt2}}};

thus, we can set in (
4.2.28
) as

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}^{*}(-\theta)=\bm{\xi}(\theta)\implies\bm{\xi}^{*}(-1)=\bm{\xi}(1)=\bm{\xi}_{\textsc{l}},~~\bm{\xi}^{*}(1)=\bm{\xi}(-1)=\dot{\bm{\xi}}_{\textsc{l}},

yielding

\displaystyle\bm{\epsilon}(\pm 1)=\pm\bm{\epsilon}^{~}_{2}

\displaystyle\implies\langle\bm{\xi}(1),\bm{\zeta}(1)\rangle=\langle\bm{\xi}_{\textsc{l}},\bm{\zeta}_{\textsc{l}}\rangle,~~\langle\bm{\xi}(-1),\bm{\zeta}(-1)\rangle=-\langle\dot{\bm{\xi}}_{\textsc{l}},\dot{\bm{\zeta}}_{\textsc{l}}\rangle.

The epsilon-conjugate of the amphospinor is extended into the amphometric space as

\displaystyle\left[\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}(\theta)\right]^{A}:=\left[\bm{\xi}(\theta)\right]^{*}_{A}\left[\bm{\epsilon}(\theta)\right]^{AB}\implies\langle\bm{\xi}(\theta),\bm{\zeta}(\theta)\rangle=\bm{\xi}^{\dagger_{\hskip-0.73616pt\epsilon}}(-\theta){\bm{\zeta}}(\theta)=\bm{\xi}^{t}(\theta)\cdot\bm{\epsilon}(\theta)\cdot{\bm{\zeta}}(\theta).

(4.2.29)

Consequently, the Higgs spinor and dotted and undotted Weyl spinors have been unified to the amphospinor with the causal group under the following diagram:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rccccc}&\theta=-1&{\longleftrightarrow}&\theta=0&{\longleftrightarrow}&\theta=1\\
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
